Audit Report (PSUs) for the year ended 31 March 2013

To ensure timely and regular recovery of EMI from the operators/ franchisees,
the proposal envisaged opening of Escrow Accounts” in banks by the private
operators/ franchisees into which the daily collections of ticket sale would be
deposited. The surplus in the Escrow Account would become payable only
after discharging the equated monthly instalments (EMI) towards the buses.
However, audit, while examining the agreements with the operators/
franchisees did not find any such provision for opening of these escrow
accounts. Besides WBTIDCL, WBSTCL and SBSTC had not developed any
other mechanism to realise the EMIs from the operators/ franchisees including
obtaining of post dated cheques. Consequently, the arrears of EMI due from
operators/ franchisees accumulated since 2009-10 and stood at हैं 41.41 crore
as on 31 March 2013 as calculated in Annexure 4.2.

The agreement with the franchisees/ operators, provide that if the EMIs and
other dues remain outstanding, PSUs have option of issuing demand notices to
pay dues within 30 days. In event of continuing non-payment of dues within
notice period of 30 days, PSUs can terminate agreements and retake
possession of buses. Despite accumulation of arrears towards EMIs, PSUs
issued notices to defaulting operators/ franchisees only in February and March
2013 for clearing entire arrear EMIs within notice period, without any results.
The vehicles had not even been seized by WBTIDCL and WBSTCL from
operators as provided in the agreements.

In reply to the inspection reports (WBSTCL & WBTIDCL), Government
stated (July 2013) that in respect of WBSTCL, operators were unable to
operate the buses because of their high cost of operation and maintenance.
The operators had approached Government for reducing monthly EMI.
Regarding WBTIDCL, the response (August 2013) was that “Escrow
Accounts” could have been reviewed if the question had arisen earlier.

Thus, due to inadequate security and failure to include escrow clause in the
agreement, possibility of recovery of € 41.41 crore towards EMI had become
remote.

This issue was brought to the notice of the Government/ Management, their
replies were awaited (November 2013).

5! An Escrow Account is a tripartite arrangement by a lease financier and lease operator with
banks to ensure that the financier is able to realise dues from the cash collected and thereafter
the operator can utilise the balance to meet expenses.

Chapter IV Compliance Audit Observations

MACKINTOSH BURN LIMITED AND WEST BENGAL
INDUSTRIAL INFRASTRUCTURE DEVELOPMENT
CORPORATION

4.8 Avoidable expenditure towards payment of service tax and interest
thereon

Mackintosh Burn Limited and West Bengal Industrial Infrastructure
Development Corporation persistently failed to bill service tax on the
recipients of their services and had to incur avoidable expenditure of

र₹ 7.98 crore towards service tax and interest thereon without possibility of
recovering the amount from the recipients.

According to Section 68 of Chapter V of the Finance Act 1994, every person
providing taxable service to any person shall pay service tax” at the rate
prescribed in such a manner and within such period as may be prescribed.
Default in payment of service tax attracts interest as well as penalty. Further,
service tax paid on service inputs for taxable services was eligible for claim of
Cenvat credit®’.

A) With a view to improve drainage and flood protection, the Irrigation
and Waterways Directorate, Government of West Bengal (GoWB) awarded
(February 2005) to Mackintosh Burn Limited (MBL) the work of
‘re-sectioning’ of Ichamati river in the district of North 24-Parganas.

Subsequently, dredging™ work was liable to service tax from 16 June 2005
onwards. However, the work order contained no clause regarding liability of
service tax.

MBL then sought and received (February 2006) clarification from the service
tax authorities (STA) that the work was taxable under the category ‘dredging
service’. However, instead of depositing service tax in time to STA, MBL
raised (March 2006) a claim on the GoWB for % one crore® towards service
tax due. It also filed an appeal to the Appellate Authority ie. CESTAT™,
Kolkata against the demand (June 2007) from STA for service tax of
हैं 2.58 crore along with interest and penalties.

However, CESTAT, Kolkata (June 2010) and subsequently the Supreme Court
of India upheld (October 2010) the demand and MBL deposited हैं 7.64 crore
towards service tax (%2.20crore), interest (© 1.11 crore) and penalty

* Sections 65(95), 65(105) and 66.

* Rule 3 of Cenvat Credit Rules 2004.

“Dredging” includes removal of material including silt, sediments, rocks, sand, refuse,
debris, plant or animal matter in any excavating, cleaning, deepening, widening or
lengthening, either permanently or temporarily, of any river, port, harbour, backwater or
estuary. [Sec. 65(36a)]

% On gross value of work of € 9.83 crore executed since 16 June 2005.

“Customs, Excise & Service Tax Appellate Tribunal.

81


Audit Report (PSUs) for the year ended 31 March 2013

@ 4.33 crore) to the STA between March 2008 to August 2011 without any
recovery from GoWB.

In March 2010, MBL got another work of ‘desilting’ of Ichamati river in a
different stretch in the district of North 24-Parganas. It sub-contracted this
work to four contractors. Despite being aware about taxability of service,
MBL while bidding (February 2010), had not considered service tax
ostensibly on the assurance of GoWB that the service being of a different
nomenclature*’ was not liable to service tax. Moreover, in case MBL had to
pay service tax, there was no clause for reimbursement by GoWB.

In April 2012, STA issued show cause notice to MBL. Meanwhile, to avoid
further interest, MBL decided to discharge liability ए हैं 3.84 crore™® through
input service tax credit of %3.25 crore paid to four sub-contractors and
deposited (November 2011- April 2012) the balance due.

Thus, despite being aware of the statutory provisions, MBL failed to safeguard
its financial interests in relying on an assurance not backed by agreement and
also delayed in depositing the statutory dues, which resulted in avoidable
expenditure of %5.84crore ie. interest (% 1.51 crore) and penalty
@ 4.33 crore).

MBL stated (September 2013) that they had received work order of second
phase of de-silting of river Ichamati before final verdict of the Hon’ble
Supreme Court of India. Further Irrigation & Waterways Department assured
them that if the job came under purview of service tax, the same would be
reimbursed. Government while accepting the facts stated (November 2013)
that following order of the Hon’ble Supreme Court of India regarding
incidence of service tax on dredging activities, MBL was to include a clause
relating to service tax in such work orders in future.

Audit observed that MBL had not received the amount of service tax deposited
from GoWB. Moreover, MBL continued to rely on the assurance and did not
safeguard its financial interest despite failure to recover any amount from
GoWB in the first instance.

B) West Bengal Industrial Infrastructure Development Corporation
(WBIIDC) establishes industrial areas or estates by constructing roads, water
supply and other amenities; leases out plots for industrial purposes; manages
and maintains these estates to earn revenue by way of rent, water and service
charges, maintenance charges etc. These services are liable” to service tax
under the categories of maintenance or repair service, commercial or industrial
construction, renting of immovable property and supply of tangible goods and
services since 1 July 2003, 10 September 2004, 1 June 2007 and 16 May 2008

57Desilting’ instead of ‘dredging’
58 Service tax: हैं 3.44 crore, interest on delayed payment: 40.46 lakh.
Sections 65(64), 65(25b), 65(90a) and 105(zzzzj).

82


Chapter IV Compliance Audit Observations

respectively. Section 66 of the Finance Act 2006 extended the liability to
services provided ‘by any other person’. WBIIDC got registered with the
STA only in August 2011. Since it did not pay any service tax before that, the
DGCEI"" initiated investigation (February 2011) on its non-payment.
WBIIDC, however, decided (August 2011) to charge service tax from the
quarter ending June 2011. To avoid the imposition of penalty, it had to
deposit (October 2011) %2.14 crore towards service tax (% 1.51 crore) and
interest ( 0.63 crore) for the period from April 2006 to March 2011 from its
own resources.

Thus, WBIIDC’s lack of knowledge of statutory provisions led to non-
recovery of service tax of % 1.51 crore and interest of % 0.63 crore on delayed
deposit i.e. avoidable expenditure of € 2.14 crore.

WBIIDC stated (November 2011) that non-payment of service tax was due to
their lack of proper knowledge of rules. Audit observed (February 2013) that
WBIIDC had charged service tax only from the quarter ending
September 2011 for reasons not on record. WBIIDC accepted that service tax
for the arrear period could not be recovered and assumed all collections made
till September 2011 to be inclusive of service tax. WBIIDC accepted
(July 2013) the observation and added that since possibility of recovery was
remote, the service tax was paid to reduce the total arrear tax liability, which
would otherwise have been higher.

Government while endorsing the views of WBIIDC accepted (November
2013) the observation and added that the services rendered by it had become
liable to tax since 2006-07. But, this amendment had not been brought to their
notice although correspondence was resting with STA. Service tax was
calculated and paid on claims raised between 2006-11, considering them
inclusive of tax. This had reduced the arrear service tax demand and interest
thereon.

WBIIDC had made reference to STA in January 2005 itself and received
clarification in February 2005 while it had become liable to service tax from
2006-07.

Thus, in both PSUs, avoidable expenditure of % 7.98 crore on service tax,
interest thereon and penalty was incurred due to lack of adherence to statutory
provisions.

‘'Directorate General of Central Excise Intelligence, Kolkata Zonal Unit.

CALCUTTA STATE TRANSPORT CORPORATION

49 Excess payment of incentive to crew members of buses on long-
distance service routes

‘Calcutta State Transport Corporation continued to pay incentives on
[LDS routes based on erroneous formula which led to excess payment of
incentive to its crews.

Calcutta State Transport Corporation (CSTC) operates long distance services
(LDS) of buses in the State through its depots in and around Kolkata. In
January 2002, CSTC’s Board of Directors (BoD) revised the existing incentive
scheme for certain categories of employees including bus crew (drivers and
conductors) making incentives applicable only on productivity above 60 per
cent load factor™. The scheme envisaged a floor or base level of earnings per
kilometre’ (EPKM) and fixation of incentive slabs depending on actual
EPKM achieved on each trip. Subsequently, to encourage employees to
achieve higher productivity, CSTC decided (January 2003) that Higher
Productivity Reward (HPR) would be paid in addition to incentive, if EPKM
actually earned exceeded maximum EPKM slab. HPR would be paid as a
multiple of actual EPKM earned.

As of February 2013, CSTC’s incentive slabs for crew members™ of LDS
routes are depicted in Table 4.4.

Table 4.4: Incentive slabs for drivers and conductors of LDS routes
(Amount in 2)

EPKM on 60 per cent load factor Rates of incentive
12.00 50.00
12.50 60.00
13.00 75.00
13.50 100.00

Source: Incentive order issued by CSTC

HPR would be applicable when EPKM exceeds % 13.50. Maximum
permissible outgo towards incentive and HPR was हैं 225 for every scheduled
trip completed.

Since incentive was based on EPKM, sales of tickets are a significant aspect.
CSTC has three ways of issuing tickets for LDS viz. “agents’ sales®”,
“advance/ counter sales” and “route/ conductor sales”. As per CSTC’s
decisions, incentives are payable only on sales by conductors on LDS, as no

® Load factor is the capacity utilisation of a bus measured as the percentage of passengers
carried to seating capacity.

© Total sales divided by effective kilometres run.

“ Drivers and conductors.

65 Tickets issued by agents at commission of around five per cent.

66 Tickets issued from CSTC’s booking counters.

® Tickets issued by the conductor during a trip to passengers boarding enroute.

84


Chapter IV Compliance Audit Observations

additional effort are being put by crew members for issuing/ selling tickets
through agents and counters.

Audit observed® (April 2013) that between January 2010 and February 2013,
CSTC had earned total revenue of % 48.25 crore™ on LDS ticket sales. Of
this, 66 per cent was “conductor sales”, 31 per cent “agents” sales” and three
per cent “advance/ counter sales”. During the period, incentive paid to
employees was %1.96crore calculated on the basis of total sales
(₹ 48.25 crore), instead of entitlement on route/ conductor sales
@ 31.67 crore).

Thus, incentive to crew members under EPKM scheme was paid in excess by
₹ 1.44 crore (Annexure 4.3).

After being pointed out by audit, CSTC accepted (August 2013) the audit
observation and assured to “take steps to arrest this excess payment”.
Management further stated that process of reviewing of incentive pattern had
already started and matter would be put up to BoD at next meeting to revise
incentive pattern/ formula to prevent drainage on account of extra payment.
Government endorsed the reply in November 2013.

WEST BENGAL STATE WAREHOUSING CORPORATION

4.10 Inadequacies in recovery system resulting in loss of warehousing
charges

West Bengal State Warehousing Corporation did not raise warehousing

charges on importers in time and seek permission from Customs

Authority to sell goods before expiry of bonding period resulting in loss of
warehousing charges of € 3.30 crore.

Under Section 49 of Customs Act, 1962 (Act), where in case of any
imported goods, whether dutiable or not, entered for home consumption,
Customs Authority (CA) is satisfied on application of importer that goods
cannot be cleared within a reasonable time, the goods may, pending clearance,
be permitted to be stored in a public warehouse.

Under Section 63 of Act ibid, the owner of warehoused goods shall pay to the
warehouse-keeper, rent and warehouse charges at specified rates and if any
rent or warehouse charges are not paid within ten days from the date when
they become due, the warehouse-keeper may after notice to owner of the

65 Monthly total sales report of LDS routes.
© Conductor sales: € 31.67 crore, agent sales : ₹ैं 14.95 crore, counter sales : € 1.63 crore.

85


Audit Report (PSUs) for the year ended 31 March 2013

warchoused goods and with permission of CA sell sufficient portion of goods
to recover rent. Period of warehousing of such goods is one year”.

Further, under Section 72(2) of Act ibid, if any owner fails to pay any amount
demanded against duties, penalties, rent, interest etc. for goods un-removed or
un-cleared from warehouse after expiration of warehousing period, CA may
sell such goods after giving notice to owner. The manner of allocation of sale
proceeds for abandonment/ confiscated goods sold by CA would be adjusted
first against its entire duties and balance, if any, towards warehouse-keeper as
rent and thereafter to owner as recommended by the Public Accounts
Committee”.

West Bengal State Warehousing Corporation (WBSWC) maintains five”
Custom Bonded Warehouses” in Kolkata zone for storage of imported goods.
Audit observed that WBSWC had not formulated any policy to safeguard its
interest for realisation of warehouse charges by way of taking guarantee or
security from owner, in case of possible default. There was neither any
system of regular persuasion with owners by raising of bills nor seeking
permission in time from CA for selling sufficient portion of stocks to realise
arrear rent within the warehousing period as per Act.

Audit scrutiny revealed that there were 106 consignments consisting mostly of
old synthetic rags lying in two” custom bonded warehouses of WBSWC since
January 2004 to March 2012 which remained either un-cleared or stored
beyond warehousing period resulting in accumulation of arrear warehouse
charges of € 3.30 crore up to March 2012 (Annexure 4.4).

These consignments were stored by WBSWC on advance receipt of
warehousing charges for one month from owners and no follow up action was
taken thereafter. Recovery of warehousing charges was remote as with
passage of time the goods had become damaged, un-identifiable and had lost
their intrinsic value. Even if the goods were sold by CA, after adjustment of
its dues there would be little left, if any, for WBSWC to meet its dues.

Government stated (November 2013) that existing rules of WBSWC allow
them to realise storage charges from the importer depositor only at time of
delivery after clearing customs duty. Hence, WBSWC was unable to frame
separate policy/ legal bindings regarding realisation of storage charges
from non-moving stocks and added that they continuously pursued the

70 Capital goods/ other than capital goods intended for hundred per cent export oriented
undertakings can be stored for five/ three years.

7! 28% Report of 3% Lok Sabha.

72 Taratala I, Taratala IT, Hide Road, Paharpur and Beliaghata

* A public or privately owned warehouse where dutiable goods are stored pending payment of
duty or removal under bond. Storage or delivery of goods are under supervision of Customs
officers and if warehouse is privately owned the keeper has to enter into a bond as indemnity
in respect of the goods deposited which may not be delivered without release from Customs.

™ Warehouse at Taratala II was gutted, Taratala I was not storing custom bonded goods and
there is dispute between owner and WBSWC for Belighata warehouse.

86


Chapter IV Compliance Audit Observations

matter with CA and the latter failed to dispose off old stocks on the plea of the
goods being associated with court cases.

However, Sec. 63 of Customs Act specifies action to be taken by warehouse-
keeper in case of non-receipt of warehouse charges within due date. It was
observed that for these consignments stored in WBSWC’s godowns, CA did
not accord permission to WBSWC to sell the goods as those had become
‘time-expired’. CA had also stated (July 2007) that position would have been
different if WBSWC had sought permission to sell the goods before those had
become ‘time-expired’.

Thus, WBSWC failed to realise warehousing charges of %3.30 crore from
importers in time or seek permission from Customs Authorities to sell goods
before expiry of warehousing period.

was too high. Cost of computer hardware,
APL service connection charge included in

Mahakuma 4.4.2008 Computer hardware cost included, APL
Parishad service connection charges had been
included against the guideline. Deletion of
inhabited villages without any
justification, unreconciled data was
submitted, detail estimate in respect of
33/ 11 kv substation was not submitted.
Area distribution maps for individual area
was not submitted.

Malda 18.03.2006/ | Cost of village too high. Sub-Station was
4,4.2008 considered in those blocks where sub-
station already existed. APL service
connection charges and commercial
service connection charge was included in
the DPR in deviation of the RGGVY
guideline. The DPR had not been
prepared as per Census 2001. DPR
considered habitations with less than
300 households (without certifying
deserts/ forests/ hills/ tribal areas) i.e.
omitted habitations with more than 100
but less than 300 households.

were not given. Sub-station was
considered where already sub-station
exists. Duplication of villages,
considering of wrong Census code. Cost
per village was very high, cost of
computer hardware was included. 33 KV
line diagram and block power map was not
included in DPR.
